- **[a specific person as object](days/d17.html#term-specific-person-object)** — Day 17, Modern language · Spanish: the personal a. The personal a appears when the object is a particular person you have in mind, including names, family words used of real people, and pronouns for people.
- **[acabar de + infinitive](days/d38.html#term-acabar-de-infinitive)** — Day 38, Modern language · Spanish: acabar de + infinitive. A present-tense pattern that means ‘to have just done’ something: acabar (conjugated in the present) + de + infinitive.
- **[adjective with ser or estar](days/d13.html#term-adjective-sense-shift)** — Day 13, Modern language · Spanish: ser and estar contrasted. Some adjectives change their sense according to the verb: ser + adjective names a characteristic; estar + adjective names a condition.
- **[antes de and después de, with an infinitive](days/d53.html#term-antes-de-and-despues-de-with-an-infinitive)** — Day 53, Modern language · Spanish: antes de and después de + infinitive. Two present-tense time phrases: antes de + infinitive (before doing something) and después de + infinitive (after doing something).
- **[aquí, ahí, allí](days/d43.html#term-aqui-ahi-alli)** — Day 43, Modern language · Spanish: aquí, ahí, and allí. Three present-tense place words: aquí, here, near the speaker; ahí, there, often a middle distance; allí, over there, farther away.
- **[article](days/d02.html#term-article)** — Day 2, Modern language · Spanish: nouns, gender and articles. The small word placed before a noun (el, la; later un, una) that must agree with the noun’s gender and number.
- **[ayer, anoche, el lunes pasado](days/d64.html#term-ayer-anoche-el-lunes-pasado)** — Day 64, Modern language · Spanish: ayer, anoche, and el lunes pasado, with the preterite. Spanish time words that place a completed action in the past, and so often accompany the preterite: ayer, “yesterday”; anoche, “last night”; el lunes pasado, “last Monday” (and likewise el martes pasado, and so on).
- **[background and a completed event](days/d112.html#term-background-and-a-completed-event)** — Day 112, Modern language · Spanish: an imperfect background and a preterite event. The imperfect can set a background, already ongoing, while the preterite names a completed event against that background, using only already-taught forms.
- **[choosing fui, tuve, estuve, or hice](days/d78.html#term-choosing-fui-tuve-estuve-hice)** — Day 78, Modern language · Spanish: fui, tuve, estuve, and hice, contrasted. Fui is the preterite of ser or ir; tuve is tener; estuve is estar; hice is hacer (with z in hizo).
- **[comparative](days/d19.html#term-comparative)** — Day 19, Modern language · Spanish: comparatives. A pattern that compares two people or things.
- **[composition (Spanish)](days/d10.html#term-spanish-composition)** — Day 10, Modern language · Spanish: review and composition. A short piece of connected Spanish, written from words and patterns already taught, not from a translator.
- **[contrast of ser and estar](days/d10.html#term-ser-estar-contrast)** — Day 10, Modern language · Spanish: review and composition. The choice, in every ‘to be’ sentence, between what something is (ser) and where or how it is for now (estar).
- **[dates in Spanish (present)](days/d34.html#term-dates-spanish)** — Day 34, Modern language · Spanish: dates. A present-tense way to give a date: Es el + number + de + month (for the first, often Es el primero de + month).
- **[deber + infinitive](days/d44.html#term-deber-infinitive)** — Day 44, Modern language · Spanish: deber + infinitive. A present-tense pattern: deber plus an infinitive, for “ought to / should / must” do something.
- **[demonstratives (este, ese, aquel)](days/d27.html#term-demonstratives-es)** — Day 27, Modern language · Spanish: este, ese, and aquel. Adjectives (and, with small changes, pronouns) that point: este / esta / estos / estas (this, near the speaker), ese / esa / esos / esas (that, near the listener or a mid distance), aquel / aquella / aquellos / aquellas (that, farther away).
- **[desde and hasta](days/d48.html#term-desde-and-hasta)** — Day 48, Modern language · Spanish: desde and hasta. Two prepositions already useful in the present: *desde* marks a starting point (“from,” “since”); *hasta* marks an end point (“until,” “as far as”).
- **[di](days/d93.html#term-di)** — Day 93, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of dar. Di is the first-person singular preterite of dar, to give.
- **[dije](days/d86.html#term-dije)** — Day 86, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of decir. Dije is the first-person singular preterite of decir, to say or to tell.
- **[diphthong (ie)](days/d11.html#term-diphthong-ie)** — Day 11, Modern language · Spanish: stem-changing verbs, e → ie. Two vowel sounds in one syllable.
- **[diphthong (ue)](days/d12.html#term-diphthong-ue)** — Day 12, Modern language · Spanish: stem-changing verbs, o → ue. Two vowel sounds in one syllable, spelled ue, in the stressed stem of these verbs.
- **[direct-object pronoun](days/d14.html#term-direct-object-pronoun)** — Day 14, Modern language · Spanish: direct-object pronouns. A small word that stands in for a direct object already known: me, te, lo, la, nos, os, los, las.
- **[e → i stem change](days/d21.html#term-e-to-i-stem)** — Day 21, Modern language · Spanish: stem-changing verbs, e → i. A small set of present-tense verbs (taught today: pedir, servir, repetir, vestir) whose stressed stem vowel e changes to i in all forms except nosotros and vosotros.
- **[era and fui, contrasted in use](days/d113.html#term-era-and-fui-contrasted-in-use)** — Day 113, Modern language · Spanish: era and fui, contrasted in use. Ser already has two taught past forms that must not be treated as synonyms: fui, the completed preterite, and era, the imperfect.
- **[estar](days/d04.html#term-estar)** — Day 4, Modern language · Spanish: numbers 0–30 and estar. The Spanish verb used for place and for a temporary condition, not for identity or a lasting characteristic.
- **[gender (Spanish)](days/d02.html#term-spanish-gender)** — Day 2, Modern language · Spanish: nouns, gender and articles. The class of a Spanish noun, shown by the article: el with many masculine nouns, la with many feminine nouns.
- **[greeting](days/d01.html#term-greeting)** — Day 1, Modern language · Spanish: sounds and greetings. A set phrase used to open a meeting or to be polite, learned here as a complete chunk (hola, buenos días, gracias, por favor).
- **[gustar (pattern)](days/d24.html#term-gustar-pattern)** — Day 24, Modern language · Spanish: the gustar pattern. A verb pattern in which the thing that is pleasing is the grammatical subject, and the person pleased is an indirect object: Me gusta el libro.
- **[hay / hay que](days/d26.html#term-hay-hay-que)** — Day 26, Modern language · Spanish: tener que, hay, and hay que. hay means ‘there is / there are’ (present).
- **[iba, era, and veía, contrasted](days/d109.html#term-iba-era-and-veia-contrasted)** — Day 109, Modern language · Spanish: iba, era, and veía, a three-verb contrast. Three irregular imperfects, already taught this week, are now set side by side so they are not merged: iba (ir), era (ser), and veía (ver).
- **[imperfect and preterite of regular -ar verbs, contrasted](days/d102.html#term-imperfect-and-preterite-of-regular-ar-verbs-contrasted)** — Day 102, Modern language · Spanish: the -ar imperfect, contrasted with the preterite. The imperfect of a regular -ar verb, yesterday’s first look, is hablaba, hablabas, hablaba, hablábamos, hablabais, hablaban, for an ongoing or habitual past action in this course’s first look.
- **[imperfect of ir](days/d106.html#term-imperfect-of-ir)** — Day 106, Modern language · Spanish: the imperfect of ir, a first look. The irregular imperfect of ir is iba, ibas, iba, íbamos, ibais, iban.
- **[imperfect of regular -ar verbs](days/d101.html#term-imperfect-of-regular-ar-verbs)** — Day 101, Modern language · Spanish: the imperfect of regular -ar verbs, a first look. The imperfect of a regular -ar verb uses the stem and the endings -aba, -abas, -aba, -ábamos, -abais, -aban.
- **[imperfect of regular -er and -ir verbs](days/d103.html#term-imperfect-of-regular-er-and-ir-verbs)** — Day 103, Modern language · Spanish: the imperfect of regular -er and -ir verbs. The imperfect of a regular -er verb and of a regular -ir verb uses one set of endings: -ía, -ías, -ía, -íamos, -íais, -ían.
- **[imperfect of ser](days/d107.html#term-imperfect-of-ser)** — Day 107, Modern language · Spanish: the imperfect of ser, contrasted with iba and with fui. The irregular imperfect of ser is era, eras, era, éramos, erais, eran.
- **[imperfect of ver](days/d108.html#term-imperfect-of-ver)** — Day 108, Modern language · Spanish: the imperfect of ver, with iba and era. The irregular imperfect of ver is veía, veías, veía, veíamos, veíais, veían.
- **[indirect-object pronoun](days/d23.html#term-indirect-object-pronoun)** — Day 23, Modern language · Spanish: indirect-object pronouns. A small word that stands in for the person to whom or for whom something is given, said, or shown: me, te, le, nos, os, les.
- **[infinitive](days/d05.html#term-infinitive)** — Day 5, Modern language · Spanish: present tense of -ar verbs. The dictionary form of the verb (in Spanish, the form ending in -ar, -er, or -ir), naming the action without a subject.
- **[ir](days/d09.html#term-ir)** — Day 9, Modern language · Spanish: ir and the near future. The Spanish verb ‘to go’, irregular in the present: voy, vas, va, vamos, vais, van.
- **[ir a + infinitive](days/d31.html#term-ir-a-infinitive)** — Day 31, Modern language · Spanish: ir a + infinitive. A present-tense pattern that talks about a near future: ir (conjugated) + a + infinitive, ‘to be going to’ do something.
- **[irregular comparative](days/d19.html#term-irregular-comparative)** — Day 19, Modern language · Spanish: comparatives. A small set of comparatives that do not use más: mejor (better), peor (worse), mayor (older or greater), menor (younger or smaller).
- **[irregular participles dicho, hecho, and escrito, a first look](days/d156.html#term-irregular-participles-dicho-hecho-and-escrito-a-first-look)** — Day 156, Spanish · Irregular participles dicho, hecho, and escrito, a first look. The present perfect, already taught with regular participles such as hablado, comido, and vivido, now takes three irregular participles as a first look: dicho, from decir, to say or to tell; hecho, from hacer, to do or to make; and escrito, from escribir, to write.
- **[irregular present](days/d07.html#term-irregular-present)** — Day 7, Modern language · Spanish: tener. A present-tense pattern that does not simply drop -ar, -er, or -ir and add the regular endings; the stem or the ending (or both) must be learned as they are.
- **[irregular simple future (hacer, decir, ir)](days/d126.html#term-irregular-simple-future-hacer-decir-ir)** — Day 126, Modern language · Spanish: irregular simple future of hacer, decir, and ir, a first look. Some Spanish verbs keep the same future endings already taught, -é, -ás, -á, -emos, -éis, -án, but not the whole unchanged infinitive.
- **[irregular yo form](days/d18.html#term-irregular-yo)** — Day 18, Modern language · Spanish: irregular yo forms. A present-tense first-person singular that does not follow the ordinary stem + -o pattern, while the other persons are often regular (or follow a pattern already taught).
- **[me duele and me duelen](days/d47.html#term-me-duele-and-me-duelen)** — Day 47, Modern language · Spanish: me duele and me duelen. A present-tense pattern like *gustar*: the thing that hurts is the subject, and *me* marks the person affected.
- **[near future](days/d09.html#term-near-future)** — Day 9, Modern language · Spanish: ir and the near future. A present-tense way of talking about a planned or soon-to-happen action: ir + a + infinitive.
- **[number words (0–30)](days/d04.html#term-spanish-numbers)** — Day 4, Modern language · Spanish: numbers 0–30 and estar. The Spanish words for the numbers 0 to 30, learned as language (how the word is spelled and said), not as a mathematics drill.
- **[para (core uses)](days/d29.html#term-para-core)** — Day 29, Modern language · Spanish: por and para, a core contrast. A preposition whose core uses in this lesson are purpose (in order to, for a goal), a deadline or a point in time toward which one works, a destination, and a recipient: para estudiar, para el viernes, para Madrid, para Ana.
- **[pedir and dormir in the preterite, contrasted](days/d99.html#term-pedir-and-dormir-in-the-preterite-contrasted)** — Day 99, Modern language · Spanish: pedir and dormir, a two-verb preterite contrast. Pedir and dormir, in the preterite, share one shape: the stem vowel changes only in the third persons.
- **[pedir in present and preterite, contrasted](days/d97.html#term-pedir-in-present-and-preterite-contrasted)** — Day 97, Modern language · Spanish: pedir, contrasted in present and preterite. The present of pedir, already taught, changes e to i in all forms except nosotros and vosotros: pido, pides, pide, pedimos, pedís, piden.
- **[pensar que and creer que, in the present](days/d49.html#term-pensar-que-and-creer-que-in-the-present)** — Day 49, Modern language · Spanish: pensar que and creer que (present). Two present-tense patterns for introducing an opinion: *pensar que* and *creer que*, followed by a present-tense clause you already know how to form.
- **[personal a](days/d17.html#term-personal-a)** — Day 17, Modern language · Spanish: the personal a. The preposition a placed before a direct object that is a specific person (or a pet, treated as a person).
- **[placement of the direct-object pronoun](days/d14.html#term-dop-placement)** — Day 14, Modern language · Spanish: direct-object pronouns. Before a conjugated verb: Lo veo.
- **[por (core uses)](days/d29.html#term-por-core)** — Day 29, Modern language · Spanish: por and para, a core contrast. A preposition whose core uses in this lesson are duration (por dos horas), exchange or a price (por diez euros — a classroom phrase, not a Latin sesterce), motion through or along (por el parque), and a general reason or cause (por eso, por la lluvia, at this lesson’s level).
- **[porque and por eso](days/d54.html#term-porque-and-por-eso)** — Day 54, Modern language · Spanish: porque and por eso (present). Two present-tense reason words: porque (because) introduces a reason; por eso (that is why) introduces a consequence.
- **[present of decir and venir](days/d33.html#term-decir-venir)** — Day 33, Modern language · Spanish: the present of decir and venir. Two more irregular presents: decir (digo, dices, dice, decimos, decís, dicen) ‘to say / to tell’; venir (vengo, vienes, viene, venimos, venís, vienen) ‘to come.’ Both have a first-person -go, and venir has an e → ie change in several forms, like tener.
- **[present of ir, dar, ver, hacer](days/d32.html#term-ir-dar-ver-hacer)** — Day 32, Modern language · Spanish: the present of ir, dar, ver, and hacer. Four high-frequency irregular presents, taught as a set today: ir (voy, vas, va, vamos, vais, van); dar (doy, das, da, damos, dais, dan); ver (veo, ves, ve, vemos, veis, ven); hacer (hago, haces, hace, hacemos, hacéis, hacen).
- **[present participle (-ando / -iendo)](days/d22.html#term-present-participle-es)** — Day 22, Modern language · Spanish: the present progressive (estar + -ando / -iendo). The -ando or -iendo form used with estar to build the present progressive: hablando, comiendo, viviendo.
- **[present perfect with haber, a first look](days/d151.html#term-present-perfect-with-haber-a-first-look)** — Day 151, Spanish · The present perfect with haber, a first look. The present perfect, in this first look, uses haber as a helping verb with a past participle: he, has, ha, hemos, habéis, han, plus a regular participle such as hablado, comido, or vivido.
- **[present progressive](days/d22.html#term-present-progressive)** — Day 22, Modern language · Spanish: the present progressive (estar + -ando / -iendo). A present-tense form that presents an action as in progress: estar + the present participle (-ando for -ar verbs, -iendo for most -er and -ir verbs).
- **[present subjunctive of a regular -ar verb](days/d136.html#term-present-subjunctive-of-a-regular-ar-verb)** — Day 136, Modern language · Spanish: the present subjunctive of a regular -ar verb, a first look. The present subjunctive of a regular -ar verb is formed from the present first-person singular stem, with the endings -e, -es, -e, -emos, -éis, -en: hable, hables, hable, hablemos, habléis, hablen.
- **[present subjunctive of estar](days/d143.html#term-present-subjunctive-of-estar)** — Day 143, Modern language · Spanish: the present subjunctive of estar, a first look. The present subjunctive of estar is irregular: esté, estés, esté, estemos, estéis, estén.
- **[present subjunctive of haber](days/d146.html#term-present-subjunctive-of-haber)** — Day 146, Modern language · Spanish: the present subjunctive of haber, a first look. The present subjunctive of haber, in the impersonal use already begun from hay, is irregular: haya, hayas, haya, hayamos, hayáis, hayan.
- **[present subjunctive of ir](days/d142.html#term-present-subjunctive-of-ir)** — Day 142, Modern language · Spanish: the present subjunctive of ir, a first look. The present subjunctive of ir is irregular: vaya, vayas, vaya, vayamos, vayáis, vayan.
- **[present subjunctive of regular -er and -ir verbs](days/d137.html#term-present-subjunctive-of-regular-er-and-ir-verbs)** — Day 137, Modern language · Spanish: the present subjunctive of regular -er and -ir verbs. The present subjunctive of a regular -er or -ir verb uses the endings -a, -as, -a, -amos, -áis, -an: coma, comas, coma, comamos, comáis, coman; viva, vivas, viva, vivamos, viváis, vivan.
- **[present subjunctive of ser](days/d141.html#term-present-subjunctive-of-ser)** — Day 141, Modern language · Spanish: the present subjunctive of ser, a first look. The present subjunctive of ser is irregular: sea, seas, sea, seamos, seáis, sean.
- **[present tense of ser](days/d03.html#term-ser-present)** — Day 3, Modern language · Spanish: ser and description. The forms of ser used for now: soy, eres, es, somos, sois, son.
- **[preterite and imperfect, mixed](days/d114.html#term-preterite-and-imperfect-mixed)** — Day 114, Modern language · Spanish: preterite and imperfect, mixed. A mixed practice of this week’s choice between already-taught preterite and imperfect forms, including backgrounds paired with completed events, and the contrasts fui / iba and fui / era.
- **[preterite of dormir](days/d98.html#term-preterite-of-dormir)** — Day 98, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of dormir, o → u in the third person. Dormir, already taught in the present with the o → ue stem change, has a preterite in which the stem vowel becomes u only in the third persons: dormí, dormiste, durmió, dormimos, dormisteis, durmieron.
- **[preterite of pedir](days/d96.html#term-preterite-of-pedir)** — Day 96, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of pedir, e → i in the third person. Pedir, already taught in the present with the e → i stem change, has a preterite in which the stem vowel becomes i only in the third persons: pedí, pediste, pidió, pedimos, pedisteis, pidieron.
- **[preterite of regular -ar verbs](days/d61.html#term-preterite-regular-ar)** — Day 61, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of regular -ar verbs. A simple-past tense of Spanish regular -ar verbs, used for a completed action in the past.
- **[preterite of tener](days/d76.html#term-preterite-of-tener)** — Day 76, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of tener. The preterite of tener is tuve, tuviste, tuvo, tuvimos, tuvisteis, tuvieron: a uv-stem, not the regular -er pattern of comer, and not the fui set of ser and ir already taught last week.
- **[preterite versus imperfect, a first look](days/d111.html#term-preterite-versus-imperfect-a-first-look)** — Day 111, Modern language · Spanish: preterite versus imperfect, a first look. The preterite and the imperfect, already taught as forms, are now chosen as two past tenses with different jobs.
- **[pronouns with two verbs](days/d39.html#term-pronouns-two-verbs)** — Day 39, Modern language · Spanish: object pronouns with two verbs. When a conjugated verb is followed by an infinitive (ir a, tener que, acabar de, and similar), a direct- or indirect-object pronoun may attach to the infinitive or stand before the conjugated verb.
- **[pude](days/d81.html#term-pude)** — Day 81, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of poder. Pude is the first-person singular preterite of poder.
- **[puse](days/d82.html#term-puse)** — Day 82, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of poner. Puse is the first-person singular preterite of poner, to put or to place.
- **[question (Spanish)](days/d06.html#term-spanish-question)** — Day 6, Modern language · Spanish: questions. A sentence that asks, marked in writing by ¿ at the start and ? at the end, and often begun with a question word such as qué, dónde, or cómo.
- **[question word](days/d06.html#term-question-word)** — Day 6, Modern language · Spanish: questions. A word such as qué (what), dónde (where), or cómo (how) that opens many questions and is written with an accent in these forms.
- **[quise](days/d84.html#term-quise)** — Day 84, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of querer, and a four-stem contrast. Quise is the first-person singular preterite of querer.
- **[reflexive verb](days/d16.html#term-reflexive-verb)** — Day 16, Modern language · Spanish: reflexive verbs. A verb whose object is the same as the subject, marked by a reflexive pronoun: me, te, se, nos, os, se.
- **[saber and conocer](days/d18.html#term-saber-conocer)** — Day 18, Modern language · Spanish: irregular yo forms. Two verbs that both translate as ‘to know’.
- **[saber and conocer, in the present](days/d46.html#term-saber-and-conocer-in-the-present)** — Day 46, Modern language · Spanish: saber and conocer (present). Two present-tense verbs that both translate a kind of “know,” with different jobs.
- **[ser](days/d03.html#term-ser)** — Day 3, Modern language · Spanish: ser and description. The Spanish verb used for what something is: identity, origin, characteristics, and time.
- **[ser versus estar](days/d13.html#term-ser-vs-estar)** — Day 13, Modern language · Spanish: ser and estar contrasted. Two verbs that both translate as ‘to be’, used for different kinds of being.
- **[simple conditional of a regular verb](days/d131.html#term-simple-conditional-of-a-regular-verb)** — Day 131, Modern language · Spanish: the simple conditional of regular verbs, a first look. The simple conditional of a regular Spanish verb keeps the whole infinitive, as last week’s regular future did, and adds the endings -ía, -ías, -ía, -íamos, -íais, -ían.
- **[simple future of a regular verb](days/d121.html#term-simple-future-of-a-regular-verb)** — Day 121, Modern language · Spanish: the simple future of a regular -ar verb, a first look. The simple future of a regular Spanish verb keeps the whole infinitive and adds the endings -é, -ás, -á, -emos, -éis, -án.
- **[Spanish possessive adjective](days/d41.html#term-spanish-possessive-adjective)** — Day 41, Modern language · Spanish: possessive adjectives (mi, tu, su, nuestro). A word such as mi, tu, su, nuestro, or vuestro that shows who owns something and, in the nuestro / vuestro set, agrees in gender and number with the thing owned, not with the owner.
- **[stem](days/d08.html#term-stem)** — Day 8, Modern language · Spanish: -er and -ir verbs. The part of the verb that remains when the infinitive ending -ar, -er, or -ir is removed, to which the present endings are added.
- **[stem-changing verb (e → ie)](days/d11.html#term-stem-changing-e-ie)** — Day 11, Modern language · Spanish: stem-changing verbs, e → ie. A verb whose stem vowel e becomes ie in the present, in every form except nosotros and vosotros.
- **[stem-changing verb (o → ue)](days/d12.html#term-stem-changing-o-ue)** — Day 12, Modern language · Spanish: stem-changing verbs, o → ue. A verb whose stem vowel o becomes ue in the present, in every form except nosotros and vosotros.
- **[supe](days/d83.html#term-supe)** — Day 83, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of saber. Supe is the first-person singular preterite of saber.
- **[superlative (el más / el menos)](days/d36.html#term-superlative-el-mas)** — Day 36, Modern language · Spanish: the superlative. A present-tense pattern that names the top or bottom of a group: el/la/los/las + más (or menos) + adjective + de.
- **[también and tampoco](days/d52.html#term-tambien-and-tampoco)** — Day 52, Modern language · Spanish: también and tampoco (present). Two present-tense agreement words: también agrees with a positive statement (also); tampoco agrees with a negative (neither / not either).
- **[tan… como](days/d42.html#term-tan-como)** — Day 42, Modern language · Spanish: tan… como (as … as). The present-tense pattern for equality with an adjective or adverb: tan + adjective + como, “as … as.”
- **[telling time (present)](days/d28.html#term-telling-time-es)** — Day 28, Modern language · Spanish: telling the time. The present-tense pattern for the hour: ¿Qué hora es? Es la una (1:00), Son las dos (2:00 and the other hours).
- **[tener](days/d07.html#term-tener)** — Day 7, Modern language · Spanish: tener. The Spanish verb ‘to have’, irregular in the present: tengo, tienes, tiene, tenemos, tenéis, tienen.
- **[tener que](days/d26.html#term-tener-que)** — Day 26, Modern language · Spanish: tener que, hay, and hay que. A present-tense pattern that expresses obligation: tener que + infinitive, ‘to have to’ do something.
- **[the article with body parts](days/d16.html#term-body-part-article)** — Day 16, Modern language · Spanish: reflexive verbs. When the possessor is already clear from a reflexive pronoun, Spanish usually uses the definite article with a body part, not a possessive adjective.
- **[the irregular participle puesto, a first look](days/d166.html#term-the-irregular-participle-puesto-a-first-look)** — Day 166, Modern language · Spanish: the irregular participle puesto, a first look. Puesto is the irregular past participle of poner, to put or to place, used with the already-taught helping forms of haber: he puesto, has puesto, ha puesto, hemos puesto, habéis puesto, han puesto.
- **[the irregular participle visto, a first look](days/d161.html#term-the-irregular-participle-visto-a-first-look)** — Day 161, Spanish · The irregular participle visto, a first look. The present perfect, already taught with regular participles and with dicho, hecho, and escrito, now takes one more irregular participle as a first look: visto, from ver, to see.
- **[the irregular participle vuelto, a first look](days/d171.html#term-the-irregular-participle-vuelto-a-first-look)** — Day 171, Modern language · Spanish: the irregular participle vuelto, a first look. Vuelto is the irregular past participle of volver, to return, used with the already-taught helping forms of haber: he vuelto, has vuelto, ha vuelto, hemos vuelto, habéis vuelto, han vuelto.
- **[the preterite of hacer](days/d72.html#term-preterite-hacer)** — Day 72, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of hacer. The preterite of hacer is hice, hiciste, hizo, hicimos, hicisteis, hicieron.
- **[the preterite of regular -er verbs](days/d66.html#term-preterite-regular-er)** — Day 66, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of regular -er verbs. The past-tense forms of a regular -er verb, such as comer: comí, comiste, comió, comimos, comisteis, comieron.
- **[the preterite of regular -ir verbs](days/d67.html#term-preterite-regular-ir)** — Day 67, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of regular -ir verbs, with the same endings as -er. The past-tense forms of a regular -ir verb, such as vivir: viví, viviste, vivió, vivimos, vivisteis, vivieron.
- **[the preterite of ser and ir](days/d71.html#term-preterite-ser-and-ir)** — Day 71, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of ser and ir. The verbs ser and ir share one preterite set: fui, fuiste, fue, fuimos, fuisteis, fueron.
- **[three-conjugation imperfect, contrasted with the preterite](days/d104.html#term-three-conjugation-imperfect-contrasted-with-the-preterite)** — Day 104, Modern language · Spanish: the imperfect, a three-conjugation contrast. Regular verbs in the imperfect now have three models, already taught this week: hablaba (-ar), comía (-er), and vivía (-ir).
- **[traje](days/d88.html#term-traje)** — Day 88, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of traer. Traje is the first-person singular preterite of traer, to bring.
- **[two-week Spanish check](days/d20.html#term-two-week-spanish)** — Day 20, Modern language · Spanish: two-week review and a short composition. A short, taught review and a composition that must use these two weeks’ skills: stem changes, ser/estar, direct-object pronouns, reflexives, the personal a, irregular yo forms, saber/conocer, and comparatives.
- **[two-week Spanish composition](days/d30.html#term-two-week-spanish-composition)** — Day 30, Modern language · Spanish: two-week review and a short composition. A short present-tense composition that must use these two weeks’ skills: e → i stems, the present progressive, indirect-object pronouns, the gustar pattern, tener que / hay / hay que, demonstratives, telling time, and a careful por / para contrast.
- **[uv-stem preterite](days/d77.html#term-uv-stem-preterite)** — Day 77, Modern language · Spanish: estar and andar, the same uv pattern. Tener, estar, and andar share a uv-stem in the preterite: tuve, estuve, anduve (and tuviste, estuviste, anduviste, and so on).
- **[vi](days/d91.html#term-vi)** — Day 91, Modern language · Spanish: the preterite of ver. Vi is the first-person singular preterite of ver, to see.
- **[vowel (Spanish)](days/d01.html#term-spanish-vowel)** — Day 1, Modern language · Spanish: sounds and greetings. Spanish has five pure vowel sounds — a, e, i, o, u — that do not glide the way many English vowels do.
- **[weather in the present](days/d37.html#term-weather-present)** — Day 37, Modern language · Spanish: the weather, in the present. Present-tense patterns for the weather: hace + a weather noun or adjective in set phrases (hace frío, hace calor, hace viento, hace sol); está + an adjective (está nublado, está despejado); and hay + a noun (hay niebla, hay nubes).
- **[week's Spanish close](days/d50.html#term-weeks-spanish-close-w10)** — Day 50, Modern language · Spanish: week review and a short composition. A Friday check of this week's present-tense Spanish only: *saber* vs *conocer*; *me duele* / *me duelen*; *desde* and *hasta*; and affirmative *pensar que* / *creer que*.
- **[week-3 Spanish review](days/d15.html#term-week3-spanish-review)** — Day 15, Modern language · Spanish: week-3 review (stems, ser/estar, pronouns). A short, taught review of the three skills of this week: stem changes (e → ie and o → ue, not in nosotros/vosotros), the ser/estar choice, and direct-object pronouns and their placement.
- **[week-5 Spanish check](days/d25.html#term-week-5-spanish-check)** — Day 25, Modern language · Spanish: week-5 review (e → i, progressive, IOP, gustar). A short review of this week’s present-tense skills: e → i stems, the present progressive, indirect-object pronouns, and the gustar pattern.
- **[week’s Spanish close](days/d55.html#term-weeks-spanish-close-w11)** — Day 55, Modern language · Spanish: week review and a short composition. A Friday review of this week’s present-tense Spanish (ya / todavía, también / tampoco, antes de and después de + infinitive, porque / por eso) and a short present-tense composition.
- **[week’s Spanish review](days/d35.html#term-spanish-week7-review)** — Day 35, Modern language · Spanish: week review and a short composition. A Friday review of this week’s present-tense work: ir a + infinitive; the presents of ir, dar, ver, hacer, decir, and venir; and dates (Es el… de…; primero for the first).
- **[week’s Spanish review](days/d40.html#term-spanish-week8-review)** — Day 40, Modern language · Spanish: week review and a short composition. A Friday review of this week’s present-tense work: the superlative (el más / el menos / el mejor + de); weather phrases (hace, está nublado, hay); acabar de + infinitive; and object pronouns in two places with a two-verb pattern.
- **[week’s Spanish review](days/d45.html#term-weeks-spanish-review)** — Day 45, Modern language · Spanish: week review and a short composition. A Friday review of this week’s present-tense Spanish: possessive adjectives, tan… como, aquí / ahí / allí, and deber + infinitive, in a short composition, not a new tense.
- **[ya and todavía](days/d51.html#term-ya-and-todavia)** — Day 51, Modern language · Spanish: ya and todavía (present). Two present-tense time words: ya (already) and todavía (still, or “not yet” when used with no).
## Atelier

- **[a background plane behind the two forms, in the limited palette](days/d161.html#term-a-background-plane-behind-the-two-forms-in-the-limited-palette)** — Day 161, Atelier · A background plane behind the two forms, in the limited palette, a first look. A simple background plane, such as a wall or a cloth hung as a plane, is now placed behind the already-taught cylinder and the second simple object, still in the same four paints, under the same one light, value first.
- **[a cloth study in the limited palette](days/d131.html#term-a-cloth-study-in-the-limited-palette)** — Day 131, Atelier · A cloth study in the limited palette, a first look. A cloth study, in this limited palette, is a small study of a simple fold of cloth or paper under one lamp, painted only with yellow ochre, the earth red, black, and white, and with the mixed earth hue already taught.
- **[a cylindrical form in the limited palette](days/d151.html#term-a-cylindrical-form-in-the-limited-palette)** — Day 151, Atelier · A cylindrical form in the limited palette, a first look. A cylindrical form, in this first look, is a simple painted study of a bottle or can, or another upright cylinder, in the already-taught limited palette: four paints, one light, value first.
- **[a few accents, and a stop](days/d54.html#term-a-few-accents-and-a-stop-w11)** — Day 54, Atelier · A few accents, and a stop the critique can use. A late sitting on the same two-form group: a few darker or lighter accents where the study really needs them, then a stop, so tomorrow’s critique has a finished-enough page rather than an overworked one.
- **[a few plane accents, and a stop](days/d49.html#term-a-few-plane-accents-and-a-stop)** — Day 49, Atelier · A few accents, and a stop the critique can use. One or two small, honest darks or lights at an edge or a contact, still explained by the same lamp, and a decision to stop before the study becomes a display of detail.
- **[a held simple-form study](days/d44.html#term-held-simple-form-study)** — Day 44, Atelier · A few accents, and a stop the critique can use. The same sphere and lamp, carried a little further and then stopped honestly, so that Friday’s critique has one study to look at, not a stack of abandoned starts.
- **[a mixed earth hue](days/d126.html#term-a-mixed-earth-hue)** — Day 126, Atelier · A mixed earth hue, from ochre and the earth red, a first look. A mixed earth hue, in this limited palette, is a third color made by mixing yellow ochre and the earth red, still adjusted in value with white and black only.
- **[a rounded form in the limited palette](days/d136.html#term-a-rounded-form-in-the-limited-palette)** — Day 136, Atelier · A rounded form in the limited palette, a first look. A rounded form in the limited palette is a color-value study of a simple rounded object, painted with the already-taught four paints and, where useful, the mixed earth hue, with value first.
- **[a second simple object beside the cylinder, in the limited palette](days/d156.html#term-a-second-simple-object-beside-the-cylinder-in-the-limited-palette)** — Day 156, Atelier · A second simple object beside the cylinder, in the limited palette, a first look. A second simple object, such as a box, a book, or another block-like form, is now placed beside the already-taught cylindrical form, still in the same four paints, under the same one light, value first.
- **[a simple-form critique](days/d45.html#term-simple-form-critique)** — Day 45, Atelier · Critique of the sphere, and a short plan. A short, spoken or written look at this week’s sphere: what is true about the light, what is still flat or invented, and one plan for the next sitting (often a new simple form, or the same lamp on a cube or a cylinder, still not a rushed return to the head).
- **[a small light value on the cylinder, from the same four paints](days/d152.html#term-a-small-light-value-on-the-cylinder-from-the-same-four-paints)** — Day 152, Atelier · A small light value on the cylinder, still in four paints. A small light value on the cylindrical form is a lighter touch from the same four paints, placed where the already-taught one light reaches the form most directly.
- **[a two-form critique](days/d50.html#term-a-two-form-critique)** — Day 50, Atelier · Critique of the cube, and a short plan. A short, spoken or written look at this week's cube, with a brief look back at last week's sphere: what is true about the light, what is still flat or invented, and one small plan for a next sitting.
- **[a two-form critique of the group](days/d55.html#term-a-two-form-critique-of-the-group)** — Day 55, Atelier · Critique of two forms, and a short plan. A Friday critique of this week’s sphere-and-cube group: one lamp, agreeing shadows, planes and a turn, a few accents, and a stop.
- **[a value check of the planes](days/d47.html#term-value-check-of-the-planes)** — Day 47, Atelier · The same cube, the same lamp: a value check of the planes. A short look at whether the three planes still read as flat and distinct, and whether the light plane is still the one the lamp can explain.
- **[a value check of two forms](days/d52.html#term-a-value-check-of-two-forms)** — Day 52, Atelier · The same two forms: a value check. A sitting that checks whether the light plane, a middle value, and the dark plane on the sphere and on the cube still make sense under the same lamp: not equally “gray all over,” and not two different lamps.
- **[a value pass across the features](days/d71.html#term-value-pass-across-the-features)** — Day 71, Atelier · A value pass across the features already placed. A sitting that assigns light, mid-tone, and dark to the features already placed (sockets, nose, mouth, ear), according to how each plane faces the light, without moving those features to a new place.
- **[accent](days/d19.html#term-accent)** — Day 19, Atelier · Accents and a point of focus. A small, especially dark (or, more rarely, especially sharp or especially light) touch that sits at a point of contact, a deep hole of shadow, or a decisive edge.
- **[accent (head)](days/d29.html#term-accent-head)** — Day 29, Atelier · Accents, and a likeness check against the plate or plaster. A small, dark or sharp mark — or a small true highlight — that is placed late, on a found edge or at a tight plane, after the large values of the head are true.
- **[accents (finishing pass)](days/d39.html#term-accents-finishing-pass)** — Day 39, Atelier · A few accents, and a finishing pass. A few of the darkest darks, or a small, careful light, placed late and only where the plate or plaster actually has them, after the value families already sit.
- **[angle (in a drawing)](days/d03.html#term-drawing-angle)** — Day 3, Atelier · Bargue plate: refining the contour and mapping shadow. The tilt of a line or an edge, compared with a horizontal, a vertical, or a neighboring edge.
- **[axis of the cylinder](days/d12.html#term-cylinder-axis)** — Day 12, Atelier · The cylinder: the ellipse. The imagined center line running through the cylinder, from the center of one end to the center of the other.
- **[block-in](days/d02.html#term-block-in)** — Day 2, Atelier · Bargue plate: the block-in. The first stage of a Bargue copy: the largest shapes, the big angles, and the main proportions, all in light line, with no features yet.
- **[block-in (head)](days/d21.html#term-block-in-head)** — Day 21, Atelier · The head: a first block-in. The first large-shape statement of a head: the skull as an egg or a simple solid, the tilt and turn, the center line, and the placement of the features as a group, before any finished eye or mouth.
- **[careful halftone and edge](days/d88.html#term-careful-halftone-and-edge)** — Day 88, Atelier · Careful halftones and edges, still monochrome. A careful halftone, at this stage, is a small monochrome value inside the light that states a turn without flattening the light.
- **[cast (sculpture)](days/d07.html#term-cast-sculpture)** — Day 7, Atelier · Cast drawing 1: set-up and block-in. A plaster (or similar) copy of a sculpture, used as a still, pale subject for training the eye in shape and value.
- **[cast shadow](days/d06.html#term-cast-shadow)** — Day 6, Atelier · Light on form: the egg. The shadow a form throws onto another surface when it blocks the light.
- **[cast shadow of a cube](days/d48.html#term-cast-shadow-of-a-cube)** — Day 48, Atelier · The cube's dark plane, and a cast shadow. The shadow the cube throws onto the table or the wall, explained by the same single lamp.
- **[cast shadows of the two forms, still in the limited palette](days/d166.html#term-cast-shadows-of-the-two-forms-still-in-the-limited-palette)** — Day 166, Atelier · Cast shadows of the two forms, still in the limited palette. A first look at the cast shadows that the already-taught cylinder and second simple object throw on the table plane, and toward the already-taught background plane, still in the four-paint limited palette, under one light, with value first.
- **[cast start](days/d24.html#term-cast-start)** — Day 24, Atelier · A plaster feature or a continued head, started as a cast. The first sitting on a simple plaster cast (a feature or a small head), using the same order as the Bargue head: envelope, tilt, center line, large planes, one terminator — not a hunt for “likeness” in a tiny eye.
- **[cast-shadow edge on the monochrome head study](days/d93.html#term-cast-shadow-edge-on-the-monochrome-head-study)** — Day 93, Atelier · A careful cast-shadow edge, still monochrome. A cast-shadow edge on the monochrome head study is the meeting of the already-taught cast shadow with the surface that receives it — often the cloth or the plane behind the head.
- **[center line of the face](days/d21.html#term-center-line-face)** — Day 21, Atelier · The head: a first block-in. A line drawn through the middle of the face, following the turn and tilt, from which the eyes, nose, and mouth are placed symmetrically in the head’s own plane (or clearly offset, if the head is turned).
- **[contour](days/d03.html#term-contour)** — Day 3, Atelier · Bargue plate: refining the contour and mapping shadow. The edge of a shape as you see it from the viewing point: its angles, tilts, and changes of direction, drawn after the block-in is right.
- **[control of value](days/d14.html#term-value-control)** — Day 14, Atelier · Cross-hatching and control of value. The skill of making a patch as light or as dark as you intend, and of keeping a family even where the form is even.
- **[core shadow](days/d06.html#term-core-shadow)** — Day 6, Atelier · Light on form: the egg. The band of darker value on a form, where the surface turns away from the light and before the reflected light on the shadow side begins.
- **[critique](days/d10.html#term-critique)** — Day 10, Atelier · Critique and plan. A structured look at a finished or nearly finished drawing, naming what is true (shape, value, family) and what must be corrected, with a reason for each point.
- **[critique (two-week head)](days/d30.html#term-critique-two-week-head)** — Day 30, Atelier · Critique and a two-week plan. A structured look at these two weeks’ head or plaster: largest strengths, largest remaining errors of proportion or value, and a short written plan for what a later sitting (or a later week) would repair first.
- **[cross-hatching](days/d14.html#term-cross-hatching)** — Day 14, Atelier · Cross-hatching and control of value. A second (or later) layer of hatch laid across the first, at a new angle, to deepen value or to tighten form.
- **[ear in context](days/d31.html#term-ear-in-context)** — Day 31, Atelier · The ear, in the context of the head. The ear is drawn or painted as part of the head: its height, tilt, and distance from the features already placed, judged against the plate or plaster.
- **[edge quality](days/d18.html#term-edge-quality)** — Day 18, Atelier · Lost-and-found edges. How sudden or gradual a change of value is at a boundary: razor-sharp, firm, soft, or lost.
- **[ellipse](days/d12.html#term-ellipse)** — Day 12, Atelier · The cylinder: the ellipse. The shape a circle appears to take when it is foreshortened — the end of a cylinder, or a wheel seen at an angle.
- **[eye in context](days/d26.html#term-eye-in-context)** — Day 26, Atelier · The eye, in the context of the head. A study of an eye (or a pair of eyes) as it sits in the already-blocked head: the brow plane, the socket, the lids, and the value family of the head, not a floating almond drawn first and made “alive” with a highlight.
- **[family check (grisaille)](days/d82.html#term-family-check-grisaille)** — Day 82, Atelier · Keeping the families while the lay-in dries or sits. A family check, at this stage, is a short, fixed look at the monochrome lay-in: do the light family and the shadow family still stand as two large shapes? Has a mid-tone drifted from one family into the other? Has the terminator become a line?
- **[family check after light modeling](days/d87.html#term-family-check-after-light-modeling)** — Day 87, Atelier · A family check after modeling in the light. A family check after light modeling is a pause, at a distance, to see whether the light family still reads as one lighter family, whether the shadow still reads as one darker family, and whether yesterday’s halftones have flattened the light or crossed the terminator.
- **[feature study](days/d15.html#term-feature-study)** — Day 15, Atelier · A second Bargue plate: the feature. A drawing of a single facial feature from a plate (here, a Bargue lithograph), treated as a constructed form with planes, not as a symbol of an eye or a mouth.
- **[feature triangle](days/d22.html#term-feature-triangle)** — Day 22, Atelier · The head: working proportions, checked against the subject. The group of the two eyes (or the brows) and the base of the nose and mouth, placed as one shape on the front plane of the face, rather than as three ornaments.
- **[first move from a plan](days/d36.html#term-first-move-from-plan)** — Day 36, Atelier · The first move from last week’s plan. The single, named correction you wrote at the end of last week — a placement, a width, a tilt, or a value family — now made on the study, and then checked against the plate or plaster.
- **[foreshortening](days/d11.html#term-foreshortening)** — Day 11, Atelier · The cube: planes and foreshortening. The apparent shortening of a length or a shape that recedes from you.
- **[form](days/d05.html#term-form)** — Day 5, Atelier · Bargue plate: modeling and completion. The three-dimensional shape of the subject, as it is turned by light and as it must read on a flat page.
- **[grisaille](days/d79.html#term-grisaille)** — Day 79, Atelier · Grisaille: a first look, as monochrome value. Grisaille is monochrome value painting: light and shadow stated in one hue, or in gray, rather than in full color.
- **[grisaille lay-in](days/d81.html#term-grisaille-lay-in)** — Day 81, Atelier · Grisaille: a thin lay-in of the families. A grisaille lay-in is a thin first pass in monochrome that states the large light family and the large shadow family on the head, without finishing edges or features.
- **[hair as mass](days/d32.html#term-hair-as-mass)** — Day 32, Atelier · Hair as masses, not as strands first. Hair is first drawn or painted as a few large shapes of light and dark that sit on the skull, not as a collection of single hairs.
- **[halftone](days/d09.html#term-halftone)** — Day 9, Atelier · Cast drawing 3: halftones. The middle values on the lit side of a form, between the lights and the terminator, that show the surface turning toward shadow.
- **[hatching](days/d13.html#term-hatching)** — Day 13, Atelier · The sphere, and hatching that follows the form. A way of building value with rows of strokes, rather than with a smudge alone.
- **[head on the neck](days/d33.html#term-head-on-neck)** — Day 33, Atelier · The neck, and how the head sits on it. The head does not float: it sits on the neck, which has width, a slight forward or backward lean, and a connection into the shoulders.
- **[highlight](days/d09.html#term-highlight)** — Day 9, Atelier · Cast drawing 3: halftones. The smallest, brightest spot where the light is most directly reflected to the eye; it sits inside the lights, not in the shadows.
- **[lay-in pause](days/d84.html#term-lay-in-pause)** — Day 84, Atelier · A pause on the grisaille: what the lay-in has earned. A lay-in pause is a short written account, at the end of this week’s grisaille work, of what the monochrome lay-in has already earned (clear families, a terminator that turns, one modest shadow turn) and what is reserved for a later sitting (modeling in the light, a careful feature, any later color).
- **[light and shadow families](days/d08.html#term-light-shadow-families)** — Day 8, Atelier · Cast drawing 2: separating light and shadow. The rule that values on the lit side stay in the light range, and values on the shadow side stay in the shadow range, so the two sides do not get mixed.
- **[light family and shadow family](days/d76.html#term-light-family-and-shadow-family)** — Day 76, Atelier · The light family and the shadow family, on the head. The light family is the set of values on the surfaces that still face the light (highlight and midtone).
- **[light on a simple form](days/d41.html#term-light-on-a-simple-form)** — Day 41, Atelier · Light on a sphere: highlight, midtone, core shadow. A first study of how a single, steady light falls on a simple solid (a sphere is today’s form): a highlight, a midtone, a core shadow, and, if the light and the table allow, a cast shadow and a little reflected light.
- **[likeness check in context](days/d37.html#term-likeness-check-context)** — Day 37, Atelier · A likeness check of the features in context. A slow look at whether the features, already placed, still sit in the right heights, distances, and tilts on this head, judged as a set against the plate or plaster.
- **[likeness check of the mouth and chin](days/d98.html#term-likeness-check-of-the-mouth-and-chin)** — Day 98, Atelier · A likeness check of the mouth and chin together. A likeness check, already taught earlier as a check of the features in context, is used today on the mouth and chin together.
- **[likeness check of the neck and shoulders](days/d113.html#term-likeness-check-of-the-neck-and-shoulders)** — Day 113, Atelier · A likeness check of the neck and the two shoulder turns. A likeness check, already taught as a method on the mouth, the eyes, and the ears, is now applied to the neck volume and the two shoulder turns together: width of the neck, how it sits under the jaw, height and softness of the two turns, and whether the head still sits on the neck rather than floating.
- **[likeness check of the two ears](days/d108.html#term-likeness-check-of-the-two-ears)** — Day 108, Atelier · A likeness check of the two ears together. A likeness check, already taught, is used today on the two ears together.
- **[likeness check of the two eyes](days/d103.html#term-likeness-check-of-the-two-eyes)** — Day 103, Atelier · A likeness check of the two eyes together. A likeness check, already taught, is used today on the two eyes together.
- **[likeness to the plate](days/d29.html#term-likeness-to-the-plate)** — Day 29, Atelier · Accents, and a likeness check against the plate or plaster. The match of the drawing to this Bargue head or plaster: tilt, silhouette, feature triangle, and light.
- **[limited palette](days/d121.html#term-limited-palette)** — Day 121, Atelier · A limited palette, a first look. A limited palette, as begun today, is a small set of paints used together so that color is learned through mixing and through value, not through a large store of ready-made hues.
- **[lost-and-found at the hair and ear](days/d38.html#term-lost-found-hair-ear)** — Day 38, Atelier · Hair edges and the ear: lost-and-found, again. A second, slower look at which edges of the hair masses, and of the ear they may overlap, should stay soft (lost) and which may be a little clearer (found), as the plate or plaster shows.
- **[lost-and-found edge](days/d18.html#term-lost-and-found-edge)** — Day 18, Atelier · Lost-and-found edges. A contour or an inner edge that is sharp in one passage (found) and soft or gone in another (lost), according to the light and the turn of the form.
- **[lost-and-found on the head](days/d28.html#term-lost-and-found-head)** — Day 28, Atelier · Lost-and-found edges on the head. The week-4 skill of losing an edge where values meet softly or nearly match, and finding an edge where a plane cuts clearly, now applied to the head: hair against a temple, jaw into neck, a lid into a socket, the far cheek into shadow.
- **[modeling](days/d05.html#term-modeling)** — Day 5, Atelier · Bargue plate: modeling and completion. Using value to turn a shape so that it reads as a form in light, not as a flat cut-out.
- **[modeling in the light family](days/d86.html#term-modeling-in-the-light-family)** — Day 86, Atelier · Modeling in the light family, still monochrome. Modeling in the light family, at this stage, means small, monochrome changes of value inside the light, to show a turn of the form, while the whole family remains lighter than the shadow family.
- **[modeling in the shadow family](days/d83.html#term-modeling-in-the-shadow-family)** — Day 83, Atelier · Modeling inside the shadow family, still monochrome. Modeling in the shadow family, at this stage, means small, monochrome changes of value inside the shadow, to show a turn of the form, while the whole family remains darker than the light family.
- **[nine-step scale](days/d04.html#term-nine-step-scale)** — Day 4, Atelier · Value: the nine-step scale. A row of nine even steps of gray from white to black, used so that values in a drawing can be chosen and compared.
- **[nose and mouth in context](days/d27.html#term-nose-mouth-context)** — Day 27, Atelier · The nose and mouth, in the context of the head. The nose and mouth placed as part of the feature triangle on the existing head, with the under-plane of the nose, the center line through the mouth, and their value families, rather than a separately perfected nostril or lip on a blank mid-face.
- **[one light, held](days/d42.html#term-one-light-held)** — Day 42, Atelier · The same sphere, the same lamp: a value check. The same single lamp, in the same place, so that yesterday’s highlight and core shadow can be checked and slow value can be added, rather than a new arrangement invented mid-study.
- **[overlap](days/d16.html#term-overlap)** — Day 16, Atelier · Combining solids: a small still life. One form partly hiding another, which both creates a depth cue and forces you to measure where a contour stops and another begins.
- **[plan (for the next work)](days/d10.html#term-next-work-plan)** — Day 10, Atelier · Critique and plan. A short, practical list of what to keep and what to change in the next set-up or the next drawing, written after the critique.
- **[plan for the next sittings](days/d20.html#term-next-sittings-plan)** — Day 20, Atelier · Critique and a two-week plan. A short, written plan that names one or two skills to practice, the set-up that will make that practice possible, and how you will know the sittings have done their work.
- **[plane (of a form)](days/d11.html#term-plane-of-form)** — Day 11, Atelier · The cube: planes and foreshortening. A flat face of a form, or a flattened patch used to understand a curve.
- **[planes of a cube, under one lamp](days/d46.html#term-planes-of-a-cube-under-one-lamp)** — Day 46, Atelier · A cube under one lamp: three planes. The visible faces of a cube, seen as flat planes of different value under a single lamp: typically a light plane (most turned toward the lamp), a mid plane, and a dark plane.
- **[planes of the head](days/d23.html#term-planes-of-the-head)** — Day 23, Atelier · Planes of the head, and one large terminator. A way of simplifying the head into a few large, relatively flat surfaces that catch or turn from the light — forehead, front plane of the face, side plane, jaw, and the underside of the chin — so the light can be read before small forms are carved in.
- **[plaster feature](days/d17.html#term-plaster-feature)** — Day 17, Atelier · A plaster feature: from plane to a more particular form. A small white or off-white cast of a nose, ear, mouth, or eye, used so that color does not distract and the light can be read as value.
- **[plumb line](days/d01.html#term-plumb-line)** — Day 1, Atelier · Setting up, sight-size and line control. A weighted string, or a pencil held up as a vertical, used to check which points in the subject line up one above the other.
- **[point of focus](days/d19.html#term-focus)** — Day 19, Atelier · Accents and a point of focus. The place in the drawing you want the eye to arrive at first: usually the most particular passage of form, with a slightly stronger contrast or a slightly sharper found edge, while the surroundings are relatively quieter.
- **[proportion](days/d02.html#term-proportion)** — Day 2, Atelier · Bargue plate: the block-in. The relation of one length or shape to another, and to the whole, as seen from the viewing point.
- **[reflected light in the limited palette](days/d141.html#term-reflected-light-in-the-limited-palette)** — Day 141, Atelier · Reflected light in the limited palette, a first look. Reflected light in the limited palette is the already-taught reflected light, now used on a color-value study: a softer light in the shadow, bounced from a nearby surface, painted with the four already-taught paints and their mixtures, value first.
- **[reflected light in the monochrome head](days/d91.html#term-reflected-light-in-the-monochrome-head)** — Day 91, Atelier · Reflected light in the monochrome head, already taught on the egg. Reflected light in the monochrome head is the same already-taught light in the shadow family, now judged on this study: a slightly lighter strip or turn inside the shadow, caused by light bouncing from nearby surfaces, that must remain darker than the halftones in the light family.
- **[relative value](days/d16.html#term-relative-value)** — Day 16, Atelier · Combining solids: a small still life. How light or dark a passage is compared with its neighbors, not only compared with a scale in the margin.
- **[second-pass value](days/d34.html#term-second-pass-value)** — Day 34, Atelier · A second-pass value on the whole head. A second, slower pass of light and dark over the whole head, after the first block-in, so that the features, hair masses, ear, and neck share one light.
- **[set-up](days/d07.html#term-set-up)** — Day 7, Atelier · Cast drawing 1: set-up and block-in. The fixed arrangement of viewing point, easel, subject, and light, which must not wander from session to session.
- **[sight-size](days/d01.html#term-sight-size)** — Day 1, Atelier · Setting up, sight-size and line control. A way of setting up so that, from a fixed viewing point, the drawing and the subject appear the same size, and lengths can be compared with a straight arm and a pencil.
- **[symbol versus form](days/d15.html#term-symbol-vs-form)** — Day 15, Atelier · A second Bargue plate: the feature. A symbol is the remembered sign for a thing (an eye as an almond, a mouth as a line).
- **[terminator](days/d08.html#term-terminator)** — Day 8, Atelier · Cast drawing 2: separating light and shadow. The line (often soft) where light on a form gives way to shadow: the boundary along which the surface turns away from the light.
- **[terminator (on the head)](days/d77.html#term-terminator-on-the-head)** — Day 77, Atelier · Checking the terminator, on the head. The terminator is the turning where the light family ends and the shadow family begins — the place the form stops facing the lamp.
- **[terminator of the head](days/d23.html#term-terminator-head)** — Day 23, Atelier · Planes of the head, and one large terminator. The boundary where the large form of the head turns out of the light into shadow — a soft or firm line, depending on the light and the plane, but one large event first, not a separate terminator for every feature.
- **[the ear as a volume](days/d106.html#term-the-ear-as-a-volume)** — Day 106, Atelier · The ear as a volume, still monochrome. On the monochrome head, the ear is a volume set on the already-placed side of the head, not a flat question-mark drawn on the surface.
- **[the ear, placed from the sockets and the nose](days/d69.html#term-the-ear-placed)** — Day 69, Atelier · The ear, and a check of the features together. The ear on the blocked-in head, first placed as a simple form whose height, on a level front-facing head, runs roughly from the brow or the top of the socket down toward the base of the nose, and which sits back on the side plane, not as a copied seashell of inner lines.
- **[the eye as a volume in the socket](days/d101.html#term-the-eye-as-a-volume-in-the-socket)** — Day 101, Atelier · The eye as a volume in the socket, still monochrome. On the monochrome head, the eye is a volume set in the already-placed socket, already taught in earlier feature work, not a flat almond drawn on the surface.
- **[the eye socket, as a plane](days/d66.html#term-eye-socket-as-a-plane)** — Day 66, Atelier · The eye sockets, on the blocked-in head. The bony recess of the eye, first drawn as a shallow plane and a socket, not as a finished eyelash or a bright highlight.
- **[the lids as planes](days/d102.html#term-the-lids-as-planes)** — Day 102, Atelier · The lids as planes, still monochrome. The upper lid and the lower lid, on the monochrome head, are small planes that wrap the already-seen volume of the eye.
- **[the mouth as a slot in the form](days/d97.html#term-the-mouth-as-a-slot-in-the-form)** — Day 97, Atelier · The mouth as a slot in the form, still monochrome. On the monochrome head, the mouth is a slot, or a soft change, in the already-blocked form of the lower face, not a dark line drawn on top of a flat shape.
- **[the mouth, as a form between two masses](days/d68.html#term-the-mouth-as-a-form)** — Day 68, Atelier · The mouth, on the blocked-in head. The mouth on the blocked-in head, first placed as a slit and two masses (an upper and a lower) under the base of the nose, on the mid-line, not as a lined smile copied from a photograph.
- **[the neck as a volume](days/d111.html#term-the-neck-as-a-volume)** — Day 111, Atelier · The neck as a volume, still monochrome. On the monochrome head already placed, the neck is a volume under the jaw, not a stick or a flat strap drawn after the fact.
- **[the nose, as a wedge](days/d67.html#term-the-nose-as-a-wedge)** — Day 67, Atelier · The nose, as a wedge, on the blocked-in head. The nose on the blocked-in head, first drawn as a wedge with a front plane and two side planes, under the brow, on the mid-line, not as a pair of nostrils drawn first.
- **[the rim of the ear as a plane](days/d107.html#term-the-rim-of-the-ear-as-a-plane)** — Day 107, Atelier · The rim of the ear as a plane, still monochrome. The rim of the ear, on the monochrome head, is a narrow plane that wraps yesterday’s hollow, not a wire drawn around it.
- **[the shoulder turn as a plane](days/d112.html#term-the-shoulder-turn-as-a-plane)** — Day 112, Atelier · The shoulder turn as a plane, still monochrome. The neck volume already blocked meets the shoulders at a turn, treated as a change of plane, still monochrome, not as a sharp outline drawn around the neck after the fact.
- **[the sphere as a form](days/d13.html#term-sphere-as-form)** — Day 13, Atelier · The sphere, and hatching that follows the form. A solid whose surface is everywhere curved, with no plane edges.
- **[transition of planes](days/d17.html#term-transition-of-planes)** — Day 17, Atelier · A plaster feature: from plane to a more particular form. The way one plane of a form gives way to the next: as a sharp fold, as a soft turn, or as a series of small planes that together read as a curve.
- **[turn of the form](days/d43.html#term-turn-of-the-form)** — Day 43, Atelier · The midtone, and the turn of the sphere. The slow change of value as a surface curves away from the light, especially through the midtone into the core shadow, so that the sphere still reads as a solid, not as a flat disk with a stain.
- **[two cast shadows, one lamp](days/d53.html#term-two-cast-shadows-one-lamp)** — Day 53, Atelier · Two cast shadows, on the same ground. Each form throws a cast shadow on the ground.
- **[two forms, under one lamp](days/d51.html#term-two-forms-under-one-lamp)** — Day 51, Atelier · Two forms under one lamp: a sphere and a cube. A sphere and a cube, already studied separately, now set together under one lamp, so the light plane, the dark plane, and a short cast shadow can be compared on both.
- **[two objects in the limited palette](days/d146.html#term-two-objects-in-the-limited-palette)** — Day 146, Atelier · Two objects in the limited palette, a first look. Two objects in the limited palette is a small still life of two simple forms, still in the four already-taught paints, value first, under one light.
- **[two-week critique](days/d20.html#term-two-week-critique)** — Day 20, Atelier · Critique and a two-week plan. A structured look at these two weeks’ work (cube, cylinder, sphere, Bargue feature, still life, plaster feature), naming what is constructed, what is still a symbol or a wire, and what the next two or three sittings should fix.
- **[two-week critique and plan](days/d40.html#term-two-week-critique-plan)** — Day 40, Atelier · A two-week critique, and a plan for the next sitting. A short, honest review of the study against the plate or plaster after two weeks of work on the same head: what is right, what still fails, and what the first move of a later sitting will be.
- **[underplane of the jaw (monochrome head)](days/d96.html#term-underplane-of-the-jaw-monochrome-head)** — Day 96, Atelier · The underplane of the jaw, still monochrome. If the light comes from above, the underside of the jaw is an underplane and belongs, as a starting claim, in the shadow family, already taught.
- **[value](days/d04.html#term-value)** — Day 4, Atelier · Value: the nine-step scale. How light or dark a surface is, considered separately from its color.
- **[value in the eye socket](days/d72.html#term-value-in-the-eye-socket)** — Day 72, Atelier · Value in the sockets. The well of the already-placed socket is treated as a recess: usually darker than the forehead and the brow, with a terminator where the plane turns, not a outlined almond drawn on the surface.
- **[value map of the head](days/d78.html#term-value-map-of-the-head)** — Day 78, Atelier · A value map of the whole head, still in one light. A value map of the head is a slow, whole-head statement of the light family and the shadow family, in one held light, without adding a new medium and without going back to a feature-by-feature pass as if the families had not been grouped.
- **[value on the mouth and the ear](days/d74.html#term-value-on-the-mouth-and-the-ear)** — Day 74, Atelier · Value on the mouth and the ear. The already-placed mouth, as two masses, and the already-placed ear, on the side plane of the head, receive value from the same light: the groove or turn between the two masses of the mouth is often darker than the masses themselves; the ear, set back on the side plane, is often in half-tone rather than in the full light of the front of the face.
- **[value on the nose, as a wedge](days/d73.html#term-value-on-the-nose-as-a-wedge)** — Day 73, Atelier · Value on the nose, as a wedge. The already-placed nose, treated as a wedge, takes different values on its front plane and its side plane, with a terminator at the turn, according to the same light used on the sockets.
- **[value pass (head)](days/d25.html#term-value-pass-head)** — Day 25, Atelier · Friday: a value pass on the head. A sitting devoted to organizing light and shadow on the head (or plaster) in a few values — usually a clear light, a clear large shadow, and one or two middles — rather than to adding new outline.
- **[week’s critique and plan](days/d35.html#term-week-critique-plan)** — Day 35, Atelier · Critique of the week, and a short plan. A short, honest review of the study against the plate or plaster: what is right, what still fails, and what you will do first in a later sitting — not a score, and not a new drawing begun in the last ten minutes.
- **[working proportion](days/d22.html#term-working-proportion)** — Day 22, Atelier · The head: working proportions, checked against the subject. A measured or constructed relationship (halfway, a third, an angle) used to start a drawing, then corrected against the subject.
- **[written pause after jaw and mouth](days/d99.html#term-written-pause-after-jaw-and-mouth)** — Day 99, Atelier · A written pause, after the jaw, the mouth, and the check. A written pause, already taught last week, is a short note of what was seen and what was corrected, written before another pass.
- **[written pause after the ears](days/d109.html#term-written-pause-after-the-ears)** — Day 109, Atelier · A written pause, after the ears. A written pause, already taught, is a short note of what was seen and what was corrected, written before another pass.
- **[written pause after the eyes](days/d104.html#term-written-pause-after-the-eyes)** — Day 104, Atelier · A written pause, after the eyes. A written pause, already taught, is a short note of what was seen and what was corrected, written before another pass.
- **[written pause after the neck](days/d114.html#term-written-pause-after-the-neck)** — Day 114, Atelier · A written pause, after the neck. A written pause, already taught as a method after the jaw, the eyes, and the ears, is now made after the neck volume, the shoulder turns, and the likeness check.
- **[written pause on monochrome modeling](days/d89.html#term-written-pause-on-monochrome-modeling)** — Day 89, Atelier · A written pause on the monochrome modeling. A written pause on monochrome modeling is a short, honest account of what the grisaille or the named drawing rehearsal now has: families that still read, a little modeling in the shadow and in the light, and at most a careful halftone or edge, with full color still reserved.
## Seminar (logic, music, art history, economics, electives)

- **[a building described in words](days/d68.html#term-building-described-in-words)** — Day 68, Art history · The church called Sophia, described in Procopius, Buildings I.1.20–22. An architectural first look that uses a primary verbal description, not a photograph and not an invented plan.
- **[a commemorative relief, later than the event](days/d48.html#term-commemorative-relief-later-than-the-event)** — Day 48, Art history · The Arch of Titus: a later relief, not a photograph. A carved scene on a later monument that commemorates an event, without being a photograph of that event.
- **[a deductible (invented figures)](days/d139.html#term-a-deductible-invented-figures)** — Day 139, Seminar · Economics: a deductible on invented figures, a first look. A deductible, in this invented classroom arrangement, is an amount the holder bears first when the named event happens, before the invented payment is made.
- **[a I–IV–V–I progression](days/d67.html#term-i-iv-v-i-progression)** — Day 67, Music theory · A short I–IV–V–I progression, with inversion. A short harmonic progression using the tonic, subdominant, and dominant triads of a key, returning to the tonic: I–IV–V–I.
- **[a later name, in a portrait habit](days/d43.html#term-later-name-in-a-portrait-habit)** — Day 43, Art history · A Flavian later name, and a refused invented portrait. The same source habit as in this week’s history: “Flavian” is a later family name.
- **[a march in duple time (invented figure)](days/d152.html#term-a-march-in-duple-time-invented-figure)** — Day 152, Seminar · Music: a march in duple time, on an invented figure, a first look. A march, in this first look, is a short invented figure in duple time: the beats group in twos, one-two, one-two, rather than in threes.
- **[a markup, then a discount](days/d114.html#term-a-markup-then-a-discount)** — Day 114, Seminar · Economics: a markup, then a discount, invented figures. A markup raises an invented price by a percent of that price; a discount then taken after the markup is a percent of the new, marked-up price, not of the original, and not a simple subtraction of the two percents.
- **[a short invented sequence](days/d112.html#term-a-short-invented-sequence)** — Day 112, Seminar · Music: a short invented sequence, a first look. A sequence, in this first look, repeats a short invented figure starting on a new degree, so the shape of the figure is kept and the starting place is moved.
- **[a simple budget (invented figures)](days/d144.html#term-a-simple-budget-invented-figures)** — Day 144, Seminar · Economics: a simple budget on invented figures, a first look. A simple budget, in this invented classroom arrangement, is a plan that places an invented income beside invented expenses and names the remainder, income minus expenses.
- **[a typical vertebra, from Gray](days/d115.html#term-a-typical-vertebra-from-gray)** — Day 115, Seminar · Artistic anatomy: a typical vertebra, from Gray 1918. Gray 1918, in the public-domain text used here from Theodora, describes a typical vertebra as consisting of two essential parts: an anterior segment, the body, and a posterior part, the vertebral or neural arch; these enclose a foramen, the vertebral foramen.
- **[a waltz (invented figure)](days/d147.html#term-a-waltz-invented-figure)** — Day 147, Seminar · Music: a waltz, a first look, on an invented figure. A waltz, in this first look, is a short dance figure in triple time, counted in threes, taught on an invented classroom figure in scale degrees, not on a copyrighted score.
- **[Aegean Bronze Age](days/d13.html#term-aegean-bronze-age)** — Day 13, Art history · The Aegean: Cycladic, Minoan, Mycenaean. The Bronze Age cultures of the Aegean Sea and its islands and shores, treated here in three parts: Cycladic (the Cyclades islands), Minoan (Crete), and Mycenaean (the mainland palatial centers, such as Mycenae).
- **[affirming the consequent](days/d41.html#term-affirming-the-consequent)** — Day 41, Logic · Affirming the consequent, and denying the antecedent. An invalid move: from “If P, then Q” and “Q,” to conclude “P.” The consequent Q is affirmed, but that is not enough to give the antecedent P.
- **[amount after simple interest](days/d74.html#term-amount-after-simple-interest)** — Day 74, Elective · Economics: the amount after simple interest. The amount A after simple interest is the original principal plus the interest: A = P + I, where I = P × r × t, on the original principal only.
- **[antecedent and consequent (music)](days/d82.html#term-antecedent-and-consequent-music)** — Day 82, Elective · Music: a period, antecedent and consequent. The antecedent is the first phrase of a period, often ending on V (a half cadence).
- **[Ara Pacis (conventional dates)](days/d33.html#term-ara-pacis-dates)** — Day 33, Art history · Roman art: the Augustus of Prima Porta, and the Ara Pacis. The Ara Pacis Augustae, an altar in Rome associated with Augustan peace, conventionally voted in 13 BCE and dedicated in 9 BCE.
- **[Arch of Constantine](days/d63.html#term-arch-of-constantine)** — Day 63, Art history · The Arch of Constantine, from Platner. A three-bay triumphal arch in Rome, which Platner records as erected by the senate in honour of Constantine to commemorate his victory over Maxentius in 312 CE, with completion dated to 315–316 CE by the decennalia inscriptions of the side arches.
- **[Augustus of Prima Porta (copy type)](days/d33.html#term-prima-porta-copy)** — Day 33, Art history · Roman art: the Augustus of Prima Porta, and the Ara Pacis. A marble statue of Augustus, now in the Vatican Museums, generally treated as a copy of a (lost) bronze type rather than as the unique original bronze in our hands.
- **[authentic cadence](days/d72.html#term-authentic-cadence)** — Day 72, Elective · Music: authentic and plagal cadences. A cadence that arrives from V to I: the dominant chord moves to the tonic chord.
- **[authentic cadence (first look)](days/d27.html#term-authentic-cadence-first)** — Day 27, Music theory · I, IV, and V in a major key, and a first authentic cadence. A close or ending in which V moves to I, so the dominant triad resolves to the tonic.
- **[bank (deposits and loans)](days/d64.html#term-bank-deposits-loans)** — Day 64, Economics · Credit, and a bank that holds deposits and makes loans. In this first look, a bank is a place that holds deposits (money left there for safekeeping, still belonging to the depositor) and makes loans (money it lets a borrower use now and repay later).
- **[Barbara (first look)](days/d31.html#term-barbara-first-look)** — Day 31, Logic · The syllogism: a first look at Barbara. The traditional name for one valid syllogism in the first figure: All M are P; All S are M; therefore All S are P.
- **[Baroco](days/d91.html#term-baroco)** — Day 91, Elective · Logic: Baroco, a second-figure AOO syllogism. Baroco is a second-figure syllogism of the form AOO: a universal affirmative premise, a particular negative premise, and a particular negative conclusion, with the middle term as the predicate of both premises.
- **[binary form](days/d122.html#term-binary-form)** — Day 122, Seminar · Music: binary form, a first look. Binary form is a two-part musical form, A then B.
- **[binary form on an invented figure](days/d157.html#term-binary-form-on-an-invented-figure)** — Day 157, Seminar · Music: binary form on an invented figure, a first look. Binary form, in this first look, is a piece in two parts, called A and B, each with its own close.
- **[budget (labeled example)](days/d34.html#term-budget-labeled-example)** — Day 34, Economics & personal finance · A labeled budget (an invented example). A plan that matches expected income to planned spending, with a leftover (or a shortfall) named honestly.
- **[caesura and enjambment](days/d20.html#term-caesura-enjambment)** — Day 20, Elective · Poetry 2: caesura, enjambment, and the volta. A caesura is a pause inside a line of verse, often (but not only) in the middle, and often marked by punctuation.
- **[calcaneus and talus](days/d85.html#term-calcaneus-and-talus)** — Day 85, Elective · Anatomy: the seven tarsal bones, from Gray 1918. The calcaneus (Gray also gives os calcis) is the largest tarsal bone, at the lower and back part of the foot, “serving to transmit the weight of the body to the ground, and forming a strong lever for the muscles of the calf.” The talus (Gray also gives astragalus, ankle bone) …
- **[Camestres](days/d81.html#term-camestres)** — Day 81, Elective · Logic: Camestres, a second-figure AEE syllogism.
